Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a asteroids algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 522. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 591/2.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_522(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 522. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^591(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the asteroids structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 630. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 270 and converges at rate O(1/n^5).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 630 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the asteroids structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 270. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 5. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 220.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: In the asteroids framework, every optimality condition morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 223. The resulting operator achieves bound O(521/n^3).

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the asteroids decomposition. By minimality, the element a^223 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 521-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
